--- SCAN 001 ([Front cover]) [cover_or_flyleaf] | [ILLUM/SEAL: Dark brown blind-tooled leather binding with a central oval interlace medallion (mandorla), cornerpieces, diamond-shaped stamped tooling, and multi-line border rules.] ---

[ENGLISH DIGEST]
Exterior front cover of the manuscript bound in stamped brown calfskin leather with traditional Coptic-Islamic geometric tooling.

================================================================================

--- SCAN 002 ([Front pastedown]) [cover_or_flyleaf] ---

[ENGLISH DIGEST]
Inside front pastedown displaying severe insect damage and paper loss without readable text.

--- SCAN 003 (10r) [text_page] ---
[ORIGINAL SCRIPT HTR TEXT]
تتكلم فيها متكلم
واما تدل على الحاضر مخاطبه
فحاضر مخاطب في الاثنا اولا. نحو قولك:
ϣⲁⲣⲉ-ⲧⲉⲛⲟⲩⲥ ⲣⲉⲕⲥ

وفي الفعل الماضي
ϣⲁⲣⲉ-ⲧⲉⲛⲥⲁϫⲓ
ϣⲁⲣⲉ-ⲧⲉⲛⲥⲁϫⲓ
وفي المستقبل
ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲉⲛ
مثال ذلك لانه المخاطبين

[ROMAN TRANSLITERATION]
tatakallam fīhā mutakallim
wa-ammā tadullu ʿalā al-ḥāḍir mukhāṭabahu
fa-ḥāḍir mukhāṭab fī al-ithnā awwalan. naḥwa qawlika:
share-tenous reks
share-tenouseno
wa-fī al-fiʿl al-māḍī
share-tensaji
share-tensaji
wa-fī al-mustaqbal
mmōten

[ENGLISH DIGEST]
Grammatical exposition of Coptic verb conjugations (past, present, future tenses) with Arabic commentary and Coptic paradigms.

--- SCAN 004 (10v) [text_page] ---
[ORIGINAL SCRIPT HTR TEXT]
ϣⲁⲣⲉ-ⲧⲉⲛⲥ...
وفي الحال
فيما انتم تتكلمون
وانا اذكر هولاء الاخر وعلى ان هولائي
يتوضح ذكرهم في الرهن ::
اشتراك الافعال الماضي والمستقبل
والامر والحال بل يختص في بعضها دون
الماضي افهم ذلك ::
ⲡⲛⲁⲧⲉⲣⲟⲩ...

[ROMAN TRANSLITERATION]
share-tens...
wa-fī al-ḥāl
fīmā antum tatakallamūn
wa-anā adhkur hāʾulāʾi al-ākhar wa-ʿalā anna hāʾulāʾi
yatawaḍḍaḥ dhikruhum fī al-rahn ::
ishtirāk al-afʿāl al-māḍī wa-al-mustaqbal

al-māḍī ifham dhālika ::
pnaterou...

[ENGLISH DIGEST]
Discussion of the present continuous/present state (al-ḥāl) and shared forms across past, future, imperative, and present tenses in Coptic grammar.

[ORIGINAL SCRIPT HTR TEXT]
الْمُتَشَابِهِ بَعْضُهَا فِي الْقَوْلِ وَتَخْتَلِفُ فِي التَّفْسِيرِ
وَقَدْ نَصَحَ لَهَذِهِ الْمُقَدِّمَةِ مَا يَفُوقُ الطَّاقَةَ
فَأَشْعَرْنَا ذَلِك مَا اخْتَصَرْنَا فِي الْمُقَدِّمَاتِ الَّتِي
وَضَعُوهُمُ الْمُعَلِّمِينَ الأَذْكِيَاءِ وَهُمْ اَلرِّكَابُ قَصْرِ
وَأَبُو اَلْفَرَجِ ابْنِ العَسَّالِ وَيُوحَنَّا اَلْقَلْيُوبِيُّ وَالشَّيْخُ
الثِّقَةُ ابْنِ اَلرَّهِبِيِّ نَفَحَ اللَّهُ نُفُوسَهُمُ اَجْمَعِينَ
وَأَنَا اخْتَصَرْتُ أَيْضًا حَاجَةَ تِلْكَ الْمُقَدِّمَاتِ وَقَدْ
جَعَلْنَا فَاتِحَةَ الْكِتَابِ هَذِهِ الْمُقَدِّمَةَ الأُولَى
الَّتِي وَضَعَهَا الشَّيْخُ اللَّبِيبُ السَّمَنُّودِيُّ وَتَلِيهَا
الْكِتَابَةُ وَكَانَ اخْتِصَارِي فِي الْمُقَدِّمَاتِ الَّتِي
ذَكَرْنَا الأَرْبَعَةُ وَالْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ تَعَالَى هُوَ الْمُعِينُ
وَالْمُرْشِدُ عَلَى الصَّلَاحِ وَلاَ خَيَّبَ الْمُتَوَكِّلِينَ عَلَيْهِ
لَهُ الْمَجْدُ دَائِمًا

Folio 70v (numbered Ⲟ in Coptic at the top left and headed by a small red-and-black ornamental cross with ⲓ̅ⲥ̅ ⲭ̅ⲥ̅) concludes the previous section with treasury, bench, and long table, before introducing a new rubricated chapter on 'Names of Buildings and Structures' (asmā mabānī wa-ʿamāyir). The Bohairic Coptic and Arabic lexicon lists terms for cities, districts, fields, fortresses, palaces, moats, bridges, towers, walls, battlements, council halls, vestibules, and vaulted halls (īwān).

Folio 71r continues the Bohairic Coptic-Arabic rhyming lexicon (al-Sullam al-muqaffā) under the chapter of words ending in the Coptic letter rho (-ⲣ), arranged roughly alphabetically by initial letter from epsilon through nu. It pairs Coptic verbs, nouns, and Greek loanwords (such as kosmokratōr, kratēr, and mitatōr) with their Arabic equivalents, separated by rubricated dots, beneath a small top-center rosette ornament flanked by nomina sacra.

[ROMAN TRANSLITERATION]
pijenephōr · al-saṭḥ | pijinior · al-ʿabd | pihir · al-zuqāqu · al-shāriʿu | pipraktōr · al-mustakhrij | pibashor · al-thaʿlab | pishchour · al-khātam | pikherkher · al-lahw | piaēr · al-hawā · pimour · al-ribāṭ | pibakshar · al-dabbāgh | pirētōr · al-tarjumān al-khaṭīb | piamēr · al-ḥaṣan | pikharaktēr · al-shakhṣ al-shabah | pikhrour · al-ḍifdaʿ | pipousher · al-bāzī | pikouper · al-ḥinnā | pisōtēr · mukhalliṣ | pishphēr · ṣadīq ṣāḥib

[ENGLISH DIGEST]
Folio 72r presents seventeen lines of Bohairic Coptic nouns prefixed with the masculine singular definite article pi- and ending in rho (-ⲣ), paired with their vocalized Arabic glosses and separated by rubricated punctuation marks. Entries include roof, street/alley, tax collector (praktōr), fox, ring, tanner, orator (rhētōr), figure/character (kharaktēr, with an Arabic marginal gloss al-shabah), frog, henna (kouper), and savior (sōtēr).

[ROMAN TRANSLITERATION]
72 | semour · tarbuṭ · sekhar tatawakhkhar | yper min ajli · fjor qawī | hanahōr · kunūz | hankour · ṭarash ṣamam | hansiour · khiṣyān | hanaukhar · ghalāʾ | hanhthōr · khayl | hor · khawf | jinchōr · waznāt wazna | chishemēr · ikhtamara | tier · aṣnaʿ | tinashorsher · ahtamm | S bāb | agapēs al-maḥabba | agios quddūs azētos Ashdūd | athanatos alladhī lā yamūt | aijos qultu spoudēs bi-ijtihād

[ENGLISH DIGEST]
Folio 72v, numbered 72 (ⲟ̅ⲃ̅) in Coptic numerals at the top left, concludes the chapter of words ending in rho (-ⲣ) with entries beginning in sēmma, ypsilon, fēi, hōri (with enlarged loops accented by red dots), janja, chima, and ti. Below a black dividing line, a rubricated heading introduces the chapter of words ending in sēmma (bāb ⲥ), beginning alphabetically with alpha entries including agapēs (love), azētos (Ashdod), agios (holy), athanatos (immortal), spoudēs (with diligence), and aijos (I said).
